These are lexical static-analysis metrics, calculated from physical source lines. Cyclomatic complexity starts each procedure at 1 and adds decisions for IF, ELSE IF, DO, SELECT CASE, CASE, WHERE, and FORALL. Cognitive complexity uses the same decisions with a nesting penalty. Argument counts are parsed from the procedure header, including continuation lines. Because it is not a full Fortran parser, use the complexity values for comparative analysis; compiler-aware tooling is required for audit-grade results.

Definitions: fan-out counts distinct names a procedure calls (CALL statements plus references matching a function defined in this source tree); fan-in counts distinct procedures calling a name, shared by same-named definitions (see the Defs column). Module coupling: Ca = units that USE the module, Ce = distinct modules USEd anywhere in it, instability I = Ce/(Ca+Ce). Internal-call ratio = resolved calls staying in the file; shared-dependency ratio = procedure pairs in the file sharing a USE module or COMMON block. Resolution is by name, not scope: an array reference shadowing a function name counts as a call, and generic or same-named procedures are merged. Use these for comparative analysis, not compiler-grade dependency extraction.

5. Dependency Cycles

Strongly connected components in the module USE graph and the resolved call graph. Any cycle is a knot that blocks extracting its members independently; modernization should break these first.

Module USE Cycles

No module USE cycles found.

Call-Graph Cycles

No call-graph cycles found (mutual recursion absent).

Call resolution is by name (see the coupling caveats); a shadowing array reference can create a false edge.
